  "new_string": "async def _setup_lcm_entry(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    lock_entity_id: str,\n    slots: dict[str, dict],\n) -> MockConfigEntry:\n    \"\"\"Set up a full LCM config entry with real platform entities.\"\"\"\n    # Build usercodes dict from slots config for the mock\n    usercodes = {int(k): v.get(CONF_PIN, \"\") for k, v in slots.items()}\n\n    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(\n        domain=DOMAIN,\n        data={\n            CONF_LOCKS: [lock_entity_id],\n            CONF_SLOTS: slots,\n        },\n        unique_id=f\"duplicate_code_test_{lock_entity_id}\",\n    )\n    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n    with patch.object(\n        ZWaveJSLock, \"async_get_usercodes\", return_value=usercodes\n    ):\n        await hass.config_entries.async_setup(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n    return lcm_entry"

{

  "old_string": "async def _setup_lcm_entry(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    lock_entity_id: str,\n    slots: dict[str, dict],\n) -> MockConfigEntry:\n    \"\"\"Set up a full LCM config entry with real platform entities.\"\"\"\n    # Build usercodes dict from slots config for the mock\n    usercodes = {int(k): v.get(CONF_PIN, \"\") for k, v in slots.items()}\n\n    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(\n        domain=DOMAIN,\n        data={\n            CONF_LOCKS: [lock_entity_id],\n            CONF_SLOTS: slots,\n        },\n        unique_id=f\"duplicate_code_test_{lock_entity_id}\",\n    )\n    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n    with patch.object(\n        ZWaveJSLock, \"async_get_usercodes\", return_value=usercodes\n    ):\n        await hass.config_entries.async_setup(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n    return lcm_entry",
  "new_string": "async def _setup_lcm_entry(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    lock_entity_id: str,\n    slots: dict[str, dict],\n) -> MockConfigEntry:\n    \"\"\"Set up a full LCM config entry with real platform entities.\n\n    Mocks ZWaveJSLock methods that need a real Z-Wave connection so the\n    coordinator can complete its initial refresh and entities become available.\n    The mocks remain active for the lifetime of the config entry.\n    \"\"\"\n    usercodes = {int(k): v.get(CONF_PIN, \"\") for k, v in slots.items()}\n\n    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(\n        domain=DOMAIN,\n        data={\n            CONF_LOCKS: [lock_entity_id],\n            CONF_SLOTS: slots,\n        },\n        unique_id=f\"duplicate_code_test_{lock_entity_id}\",\n    )\n    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n    await hass.config_entries.async_setup(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n    await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n    # Patch the lock instance's methods so the coordinator doesn't hit Z-Wave\n    runtime_data: LockCodeManagerConfigEntryData = lcm_entry.runtime_data\n    lock_instance = runtime_data.locks[lock_entity_id]\n    lock_instance.async_get_usercodes = AsyncMock(return_value=usercodes)\n    lock_instance.async_hard_refresh_codes = AsyncMock(return_value=usercodes)\n\n    # Trigger a refresh now that the mock is in place\n    if lock_instance.coordinator:\n        await lock_instance.coordinator.async_request_refresh()\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n    return lcm_entry"
}

The root issue is `_get_usercodes_from_cache` calls `get_usercodes(self.node)` which needs real Z-Wave data. I should patch the `get_usercodes` import used by the module:

> TOOL

id: toolu_01BQ4K8pENRBQt17qJfK4Pb6
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/tests/providers/test_zwave_js.py",
  "old_string": "async def _setup_lcm_entry(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    lock_entity_id: str,\n    slots: dict[str, dict],\n) -> MockConfigEntry:\n    \"\"\"Set up a full LCM config entry with real platform entities.\n\n    Mocks ZWaveJSLock methods that need a real Z-Wave connection so the\n    coordinator can complete its initial refresh and entities become available.\n    The mocks remain active for the lifetime of the config entry.\n    \"\"\"\n    usercodes = {int(k): v.get(CONF_PIN, \"\") for k, v in slots.items()}\n\n    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(\n        domain=DOMAIN,\n        data={\n            CONF_LOCKS: [lock_entity_id],\n            CONF_SLOTS: slots,\n        },\n        unique_id=f\"duplicate_code_test_{lock_entity_id}\",\n    )\n    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n    await hass.config_entries.async_setup(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n    await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n    # Patch the lock instance's methods so the coordinator doesn't hit Z-Wave\n    runtime_data: LockCodeManagerConfigEntryData = lcm_entry.runtime_data\n    lock_instance = runtime_data.locks[lock_entity_id]\n    lock_instance.async_get_usercodes = AsyncMock(return_value=usercodes)\n    lock_instance.async_hard_refresh_codes = AsyncMock(return_value=usercodes)\n\n    # Trigger a refresh now that the mock is in place\n    if lock_instance.coordinator:\n        await lock_instance.coordinator.async_request_refresh()\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n    return lcm_entry",
  "new_string": "async def _setup_lcm_entry(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    lock_entity_id: str,\n    slots: dict[str, dict],\n) -> MockConfigEntry:\n    \"\"\"Set up a full LCM config entry with real platform entities.\n\n    Mocks the Z-Wave JS ``get_usercodes`` utility so the coordinator can\n    complete its initial refresh without a real Z-Wave connection.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Build mock usercode cache entries from slots config\n    cache_entries = [\n        {\n            \"code_slot\": int(k),\n            \"usercode\": v.get(CONF_PIN, \"\"),\n            \"in_use\": bool(v.get(CONF_PIN)),\n        }\n        for k, v in slots.items()\n    ]\n\n    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(\n        domain=DOMAIN,\n        data={\n            CONF_LOCKS: [lock_entity_id],\n            CONF_SLOTS: slots,\n        },\n        unique_id=f\"duplicate_code_test_{lock_entity_id}\",\n    )\n    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n    with patch(\n        \"custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercodes\",\n        return_value=cache_entries,\n    ):\n        await hass.config_entries.async_setup(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n    return lcm_entry"
}

> AGENT

Still hanging at setup. The `mock_get_usercodes` fixture patches `get_usercodes` but there's also a `get_usercode` (singular) call in `async_set_usercode` and `code_slot_in_use`. Plus the sync logic tries to call `async_set_usercode`/`async_clear_usercode` which make Z-Wave service calls. The problem is the sync loop — the coordinator sees codes that don't match and tries to sync them, which causes more Z-Wave calls that block.

The simplest fix is to also mock `async_is_connection_up` to return `False` after initial setup so the coordinator doesn't try to sync:

Actually, let me rethink. The mock fixture approach keeps `get_usercodes` returning the same data, but the sync logic sees the switch is ON with a PIN, tries to set the code on the lock via `async_set_usercode` which calls the Z-Wave service — and that service call may be blocking. Perfect! Now I have all the information needed. Let me create a comprehensive summary:

## Comprehensive Analysis: Coordinator Sync Loop Logic

Based on my thorough analysis of the codebase, here's exactly how the sync loop works and the data formats involved:

### 1. COORDINATOR DATA STRUCTURE

**Type:** `dict[int, int | str]`

The coordinator stores a dictionary where:
- **Keys**: Code slot numbers (integer, e.g., 1, 2, 3, etc.)
- **Values**: Either:
  - `""` (empty string) = code slot is cleared/not in use
  - `"1234"` (string) = the PIN code on the lock

**Example:**
```python
coordinator.data = {
    1: "1234",      # Slot 1 has PIN "1234"
    2: "",          # Slot 2 is cleared
    3: "5678",      # Slot 3 has PIN "5678"
}
```

### 2. `get_usercodes()` RETURN FORMAT

**Location:** `providers/zwave_js.py`, line 676-730

**Return Type:** `dict[int, int | str]`

From `async_get_usercodes()`:
```python
async def async_get_usercodes(self) -> dict[int, int | str]:
    """Get dictionary of code slots and usercodes."""
    # ...
    data: dict[int, int | str] = {}
    # ...
    for slot in slots:
        code_slot = int(slot["code_slot"])
        usercode: str = slot["usercode"] or ""
        in_use: bool | None = slot["in_use"]

        if not in_use:
            data[code_slot] = ""
        # Resolve PIN (handles masked codes), skip if unresolvable
        elif resolved := self._resolve_pin_if_masked(usercode, code_slot):
            data[code_slot] = resolved

    return data
```

**Exact Return Example:**
```python
{
    1: "1234",  # Slot is in_use=True, usercode="1234"
    2: "",      # Slot is in_use=False (cleared)
    3: "5678",  # Slot is in_use=True, usercode="5678"
}
```

**Data Source:** This comes from the Z-Wave JS library's `get_usercodes(node)` function which returns a list of `CodeSlot` TypedDict objects:

```python
# From zwave_js_server/util/lock.py
class CodeSlot(TypedDict, total=False):
    """Represent a code slot."""
    code_slot: int  # required
    name: str  # required
    in_use: bool | None  # required
    usercode: str | None
```

### 3. `get_usercode()` RETURN FORMAT

**Location:** zwave_js_server library (imported in zwave_js.py)

**Return Type:** `CodeSlot` (TypedDict)

**Return Structure:**
```python
{
    "code_slot": 1,
    "name": "Slot 1",
    "in_use": True,           # None | True | False
    "usercode": "1234" | None # Can be None or the actual code
}
```

**Usage in Z-Wave JS provider (line 582):**
```python
if (current := get_usercode(self.node, code_slot)).get("in_use"):
    current_code = str(current.get("usercode", ""))
```

### 4. SYNC LOOP LOGIC IN BINARY SENSOR

**Location:** `binary_sensor.py`, `LockCodeManagerCodeSlotInSyncEntity` class

**The sync loop compares 3 sources of truth:**

**A. Slot State Resolution (_SlotState) - Line 440-500**

The sync entity gathers state from 4 separate entities:
```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _SlotState:
    active_state: str          # "on" or "off" from ATTR_ACTIVE binary sensor
    pin_state: str             # PIN value from CONF_PIN text entity
    name_state: str | None     # Name from CONF_NAME text entity
    code_state: str            # Current code from ATTR_CODE sensor entity
    coordinator_code: int | str | None  # From coordinator.data[slot_num]
```

**B. Sync Comparison Logic (_calculate_expected_sync) - Line 423-438**

```python
def _calculate_expected_sync(self, slot_state: _SlotState) -> bool:
    """
    Calculate whether slot should be in sync.

    Active: PIN should match code on lock
    Inactive: Code on lock should be empty
    """
    # Use coordinator data if available, otherwise fall back to sensor state
    lock_code = (
        str(slot_state.coordinator_code)
        if slot_state.coordinator_code is not None
        else slot_state.code_state
    )
    if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
        return slot_state.pin_state == lock_code
    return lock_code == ""
```

**In plain English:**
- If slot is ACTIVE (STATE_ON): PIN entity value should match lock_code from coordinator
- If slot is INACTIVE (STATE_OFF): lock_code from coordinator should be empty string ""

**C. Coordinator Data Priority - Line 430-435**

The sync logic **PREFERS coordinator data** over sensor state:
```python
lock_code = (
    str(slot_state.coordinator_code)
    if slot_state.coordinator_code is not None
    else slot_state.code_state
)
```

This means:
- If `coordinator.data[slot_num]` exists → use that value
- Else → fall back to sensor's `code_state`

### 5. SYNC OPERATION (_perform_sync_operation) - Line 502-542

When out of sync, the binary sensor calls:

```python
if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
    await self.lock.async_internal_set_usercode(
        int(self.slot_num), 
        slot_state.pin_state,      # The PIN from text entity
        slot_state.name_state      # The name from text entity
    )
else:  # active_state == STATE_OFF
    await self.lock.async_internal_clear_usercode(int(self.slot_num))
```

Then calls `coordinator.async_refresh()` to verify the operation succeeded.

### 6. COORDINATOR REFRESH WORKFLOW

**Location:** `coordinator.py`

**Flow:**
1. Binary sensor detects out of sync
2. Calls `await self.lock.async_internal_set_usercode(...)`
3. Provider updates the lock and (for Z-Wave JS) calls `coordinator.push_update({slot: code})`
4. Binary sensor calls `coordinator.async_refresh()`
5. Coordinator calls `lock.async_internal_get_usercodes()` which:
   - Calls `_get_usercodes_from_cache()` (reads Z-Wave JS cache)
   - If any configured slot missing/unknown → calls hard refresh
   - Returns `dict[int, int | str]` with latest lock state
6. Coordinator updates `self.data` with the response
7. Listeners (binary sensor) notified via `_handle_coordinator_update()`
8. Binary sensor recalculates sync state with fresh coordinator data

### 7. PUSH UPDATE OPTIMIZATION (Z-Wave JS)

**Location:** `zwave_js.py`, lines 618 & 659

After successful set/clear, Z-Wave JS provider immediately pushes optimistic updates:

```python
# In async_set_usercode (line 618)
if self.coordinator:
    self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: usercode})

# In async_clear_usercode (line 659)
if self.coordinator:
    self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: ""})
```

**Why?** Z-Wave JS cache updates asynchronously via push notifications. Without optimistic updates, the sync loop would read stale cache data and retry infinitely.

### 8. DATA FORMAT REQUIREMENTS FOR SYNC

For the sync loop to consider codes "in sync", `get_usercodes()` must return:

```python
{
    slot_number: "",          # For cleared/inactive slots
    slot_number: "1234",      # For active slots with the PIN code
}
```

**Critical Detail:** The values MUST be:
- Empty string `""` for cleared slots (not None, not False)
- String representation of the PIN code (even if input was int, convert to string)
- Must match exactly with the PIN text entity value

### 9. KEY VALIDATION POINTS

The sync logic has several guards that must pass:

```python
def _ensure_entities_ready(self) -> bool:
    """Ensure all dependent entities exist with valid states."""
    for key in (CONF_PIN, CONF_NAME, ATTR_ACTIVE, ATTR_CODE):
        if key not in self._entity_id_map:
            return False
        state = self._get_entity_state(key)
        if state is None or state in (STATE_UNAVAILABLE, STATE_UNKNOWN):
            return False
    return True
```

All 4 entities must have valid states:
1. PIN text entity (CONF_PIN)
2. Name text entity (CONF_NAME)
3. Active binary sensor (ATTR_ACTIVE)
4. Code slot sensor (ATTR_CODE)

If any are unavailable/unknown, sync is blocked until they're ready.

---

This is the complete, exact data format and workflow the sync loop uses. The coordinator is the source of truth for what's on the lock, compared against switch/text entity states to determine if action is needed.

    99→   848→def async_capture_events(
   100→   849→    hass: HomeAssistant, event_name: str
   101→   850→) -> list[Event[dict[str, Any]]]:
   102→   851→    """Create a helper that captures events."""
   103→   852→    events: list[Event[dict[str, Any]]] = []
   104→   853→
   105→   854→    @callback
   106→   855→    def capture_events(event: Event[dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
   107→   856→        events.append(event)
   108→   857→
   109→   858→    hass.bus.async_listen(event_name, capture_events)
   110→   859→    return events

   113→   862→async def test_notification_event_keypad_lock_fires_lock_state_changed(
   114→   863→    hass: HomeAssistant,
   115→   864→    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,

   119→   868→    """Test that a keypad lock notification event fires EVENT_LOCK_STATE_CHANGED."""
   120→   869→    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(domain=DOMAIN, data={CONF_LOCKS: [], CONF_SLOTS: {}})
   121→   870→    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)
   122→   871→    await zwave_js_lock.async_setup(lcm_entry)
   123→   872→
   124→   873→    # Capture LCM lock state changed events
   125→   874→    events = async_capture_events(hass, EVENT_LOCK_STATE_CHANGED)
   126→   875→
   127→   876→    # Create a notification event matching the pattern from HA core tests
   128→   877→    # Type 6 = ACCESS_CONTROL, Event 5 = KEYPAD_LOCK_OPERATION
   129→   878→    event = ZwaveEvent(
   130→   879→        type="notification",
   131→   880→        data={
   132→   881→            "source": "node",

   137→   886→            "args": {

   139→   888→                "event": 5,  # KEYPAD_LOCK_OPERATION
   140→   889→                "label": "Access Control",
   141→   890→                "eventLabel": "Keypad lock operation",
   142→   891→                "parameters": {"userId": 1},
   143→   892→            },
   144→   893→        },
   145→   894→    )
   146→   895→    lock_schlage_be469.receive_event(event)
   147→   896→    await hass.async_block_till_done()

   153→   902→    assert events[0].data[ATTR_TO] == "locked"
   154→   903→    assert events[0].data[ATTR_FROM] == "unlocked"
   155→   904→
   156→   905→    await zwave_js_lock.async_unload(False)
   157→   906→

  1932→def _make_duplicate_code_event(node_id: int, user_id: int | None = None) -> ZwaveEvent:
  1933→    """Create a duplicate code notification ZwaveEvent."""
  1934→    params: dict[str, Any] = {}
  1935→    if user_id is not None:
  1936→        params["userId"] = user_id
  1937→    return ZwaveEvent(
  1938→        type="notification",
  1939→        data={
  1940→            "source": "node",
  1941→            "event": "notification",
  1942→            "nodeId": node_id,
  1943→            "endpointIndex": 0,
  1944→            "ccId": 113,
  1945→            "args": {
  1946→                "type": 6,  # ACCESS_CONTROL
  1947→                "event": 15,  # NEW_USER_CODE_NOT_ADDED_DUE_TO_DUPLICATE_CODE
  1948→                "label": "Access Control",
  1949→                "eventLabel": "New user code not added due to duplicate code",
  1950→                "parameters": params,
  1951→            },
  1952→        },
  1953→    )
  1954→

  1957→def mock_zwave_usercodes():
  1958→    """Mock Z-Wave JS usercode functions for the lifetime of the test.
  1959→
  1960→    Keeps both ``get_usercodes`` and ``get_usercode`` returning consistent
  1961→    data so the coordinator and sync logic don't make real Z-Wave calls.
  1962→    """
  1963→    with (
  1964→        patch(
  1965→            "custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercodes",
  1966→        ) as mock_all,
  1967→        patch(
  1968→            "custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercode",

  1971→        mock_all.return_value = []
  1972→        mock_one.return_value = {"in_use": False, "usercode": ""}
  1973→        yield mock_all, mock_one

  1976→async def _setup_lcm_entry(
  1977→    hass: HomeAssistant,
  1978→    lock_entity_id: str,
  1979→    slots: dict[str, dict],

  1983→    mock_all, mock_one = mock_zwave_usercodes
  1984→
  1985→    cache_entries = []
  1986→    usercode_by_slot: dict[int, dict] = {}
  1987→    for k, v in slots.items():
  1988→        pin = v.get(CONF_PIN, "")
  1989→        in_use = bool(pin)
  1990→        cache_entries.append(
  1991→            {"code_slot": int(k), "usercode": pin, "in_use": in_use}
  1992→        )
  1993→        usercode_by_slot[int(k)] = {"in_use": in_use, "usercode": pin}
  1994→
  1995→    mock_all.return_value = cache_entries
  1996→    mock_one.side_effect = lambda node, slot: usercode_by_slot.get(
  1997→        slot, {"in_use": False, "usercode": ""}
  1998→    )
  1999→
  2000→    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(
  2001→        domain=DOMAIN,
  2002→        data={
  2003→            CONF_LOCKS: [lock_entity_id],
  2004→            CONF_SLOTS: slots,
  2005→        },
  2006→        unique_id=f"duplicate_code_test_{lock_entity_id}",
  2007→    )
  2008→    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)
  2009→    await hass.config_entries.async_setup(lcm_entry.entry_id)
  2010→    await hass.async_block_till_done()
  2011→    return lcm_entry

  2015→    hass: HomeAssistant, entry_id: str, slot: int
  2016→) -> str:
  2017→    """Get the enabled switch entity ID for a slot."""
  2018→    ent_reg = er.async_get(hass)

  2020→    entity_id = ent_reg.async_get_entity_id("switch", DOMAIN, uid)
  2021→    assert entity_id, f"Switch entity not found for slot {slot}"

  2053→    # Switch should be turned off

  2056→    # Persistent notification should be created
  2057→    notifications = _async_get_or_create_notifications(hass)

Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a detailed report of the setup flow and identify potential hang points.

## Complete LCM Config Entry Setup Flow Analysis

Based on my thorough examination of the codebase, here's the complete trace of what happens during setup and the potential hanging points:

### 1. **ENTRY SETUP FLOW** (`async_setup_entry` in `__init__.py`)

The setup happens in this order:

```
async_setup_entry (line 292)
├─ Validate lock entities exist in entity registry
├─ await _async_register_strategy_resource(hass)
│  └─ Registers Lovelace UI strategy module (not blocking)
├─ Create integration device (non-async, sync)
├─ await hass.config_entries.async_forward_entry_setups(
│     config_entry, 
│     PLATFORMS  # BINARY_SENSOR, EVENT, SENSOR
│  )
│  └─ This setup happens in PARALLEL
└─ if hass.state == CoreState.running:
   └─ _setup_entry_after_start (CALLBACK - non-blocking)
   else:
   └─ Listen for HOME_ASSISTANT_STARTED event (non-blocking)
```

**Key Point**: Line 325 does NOT await the platform setups to complete. It awaits the `async_forward_entry_setups` call itself, but the actual entity creation happens asynchronously.

### 2. **PLATFORM SETUPS** (Platforms: BINARY_SENSOR, EVENT, SENSOR)

When platforms are set up, each platform's `async_setup_entry` is called:

**Binary Sensor Setup** (`binary_sensor.py` lines 62-112):
- Registers callbacks for adding entities
- DOES NOT create any coordinator or perform any I/O
- Returns True immediately

**Event Setup**: (Similar - just registers callbacks)

**Sensor Setup**: (Similar - just registers callbacks)

No await operations at this level.

### 3. **LOCK INSTANCE CREATION** (happens in `async_update_listener`)

This is called from `_setup_entry_after_start` (line 286-289):

```
_setup_entry_after_start (callback at line 266)
├─ config_entry.add_update_listener(async_update_listener)
└─ if config_entry.data is empty:
   └─ async_create_task(async_update_listener(...))
   
async_update_listener (line 406)
├─ Load new slots and locks from config_entry.options
├─ For each NEW lock (locks_to_add):
│  ├─ lock = async_create_lock_instance(...)
│  │  └─ Creates BaseLock subclass (non-async)
│  └─ await lock.async_setup(config_entry)
│     └─ **POTENTIAL HANG POINT #1: Coordinator initialization**
│
├─ Create slot entities (non-async invocations of callbacks)
└─ await asyncio.gather(*setup_tasks.values())
   └─ **POTENTIAL HANG POINT #2: New platform setups**
```

### 4. **LOCK ASYNC_SETUP** (`providers/_base.py` lines 297-347)

This is where the actual I/O happens:

```
async_setup (line 297)
├─ await self.hass.async_add_executor_job(self.setup)
│  └─ Runs sync setup() method in executor (usually empty)
├─ _setup_config_entry_state_listener()
│  └─ Sets up listener for provider config entry changes (non-blocking)
│
├─ if self.coordinator is not None:
│  └─ Already exists - just request refresh
│     └─ hass.async_create_task(coordinator.async_request_refresh())
│        └─ DOES NOT AWAIT - returns immediately
│
└─ else (first time):
   └─ self.coordinator = LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator(...)
   │  ├─ async_track_time_interval for drift detection (non-blocking)
   │  └─ async_track_time_interval for connection checks (non-blocking)
   │
   └─ if config_entry.state == ConfigEntryState.SETUP_IN_PROGRESS:
      └─ await self.coordinator.async_config_entry_first_refresh()
         └─ **POTENTIAL HANG POINT #3: First coordinator refresh**
         └─ This calls async_get_usercodes() which:
            ├─ Calls _execute_rate_limited("get", self.async_get_usercodes)
            │  ├─ await async_internal_is_connection_up()
            │  │  └─ Calls is_connection_up() in executor
            │  │     └─ **BLOCKING: If provider's is_connection_up() blocks**
            │  │
            │  └─ await asyncio.sleep(delay) if rate limiting needed
            │     └─ **BLOCKING: Rate limiting delay**
            │
            └─ Calls provider's async_get_usercodes()
               └─ **BLOCKING: If provider's get_usercodes() is slow or hangs**
      
      └─ if state != SETUP_IN_PROGRESS:
         └─ await self.coordinator.async_refresh()
            └─ Same as above - **POTENTIAL HANG POINT #3**
   
   └─ if self.supports_push:
      └─ subscribe_push_updates()
         └─ Non-async callback
```

### 5. **BINARY SENSOR ENTITY ADDED** (when entity is added to hass)

When the sync binary sensor (`LockCodeManagerCodeSlotInSyncEntity`) is added:

```
async_added_to_hass (line 615)
├─ Setup state tracking (non-blocking)
└─ await self._async_update_state()
   └─ **POTENTIAL HANG POINT #4: Initial state calculation**
   └─ This can trigger _async_update_state which:
      ├─ Waits for dependent entities to be ready
      ├─ If out of sync, performs sync operations:
      │  ├─ await self.lock.async_internal_set_usercode()
      │  │  └─ **POTENTIAL HANG POINT #5: Sync operation**
      │  │  └─ _execute_rate_limited calls:
      │  │     ├─ await async_internal_is_connection_up()
      │  │     ├─ async with self._aio_lock
      │  │     ├─ await asyncio.sleep(rate_limiting_delay)
      │  │     └─ await async_set_usercode() (provider's method)
      │  │
      │  └─ await self.coordinator.async_refresh()
      │     └─ **POTENTIAL HANG POINT #6: Post-sync refresh**
```

---

## Summary of Potential Hang Points

### CRITICAL - Most Likely Culprits:

1. **Coordinator First Refresh** (Line 317 in `_base.py`)
   - `await self.coordinator.async_config_entry_first_refresh()`
   - This calls `async_get_usercodes()` which includes:
     - Connection check via executor
     - Rate limiting sleep
     - Provider's actual data fetch

2. **Sync Entity Initial State** (Line 625 in `binary_sensor.py`)
   - `await self._async_update_state()`
   - During entity setup, this waits for dependent entities to be ready
   - Can trigger sync operations if out of sync

3. **Provider-Level Blocking**
   - `is_connection_up()` - runs in executor, provider can block here
   - `get_usercodes()` - runs in executor, provider can block here
   - `set_usercode()` - actual sync operation, provider can block here

---

## Key Differences: `async_internal_*` vs `async_set_usercode`

From `providers/_base.py`:

- **`async_set_usercode`** (line 495): Just the raw provider call in executor, returns bool
  
- **`async_internal_set_usercode`** (line 510): **WRAPS** the raw method with:
  - Connection check via `_execute_rate_limited`
  - Rate limiting delay (min 2 seconds between operations)
  - **Conditional coordinator refresh** (only if `changed=True` AND not `supports_push`)

The internal version is slower because of:
- Connection check before every operation
- Rate limiting enforcement
- Post-operation refresh for poll-based providers

---

## What Happens During Initial Setup (Virtual Lock Example):

```
VirtualLock.async_setup() (line 42 in virtual.py)
├─ self._store = Store(...)  # Create storage
├─ await self.async_hard_refresh_codes()
│  └─ self._data = await self._store.async_load()
│     └─ **IO OPERATION: Loads from disk**
│  └─ return await self.async_get_usercodes()
│
└─ await super().async_setup(config_entry)
   └─ Goes through BaseLock.async_setup() above
```

For Virtual lock, the only potential hang is if disk I/O is slow.

For Z-Wave JS or other integrations, the provider's `is_connection_up()` and `get_usercodes()` are the critical paths.

---

## Test Environment Observations:

In `tests/common.py`, the MockLCMLock:
- Has instant `is_connection_up()` that just returns `self._connected`
- Has instant `get_usercodes()` that returns from in-memory dict
- Has instant `set_usercode()` that modifies in-memory dict

This explains why tests pass - they don't experience the real-world delays that actual providers encounter.

     1→"""Lock Code Manager Coordinators."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Callable
     6→from datetime import datetime
     7→import logging
     8→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
     9→
    10→from homeassistant.core import HomeAssistant, callback
    11→from homeassistant.helpers.event import async_track_time_interval
    12→from homeassistant.helpers.update_coordinator import DataUpdateCoordinator, UpdateFailed
    13→
    14→from .const import DOMAIN
    15→from .exceptions import LockCodeManagerError
    16→
    17→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    18→    from .providers import BaseLock
    19→
    20→_LOGGER = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→
    23→class LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator(DataUpdateCoordinator[dict[int, int | str]]):
    24→    """Class to manage usercode updates."""
    25→
    26→    def __init__(self, hass: HomeAssistant, lock: BaseLock, config_entry: Any) -> None:
    27→        """Initialize the usercode update coordinator."""
    28→        self._lock = lock
    29→        self._drift_unsub: Callable[[], None] | None = None
    30→        self._connection_unsub: Callable[[], None] | None = None
    31→        # Disable periodic polling when push updates are supported.
    32→        # Polling is still used for initial load.
    33→        update_interval = None if lock.supports_push else lock.usercode_scan_interval
    34→        super().__init__(
    35→            hass,
    36→            _LOGGER,
    37→            name=f"{DOMAIN} {lock.lock.entity_id}",
    38→            update_method=self.async_get_usercodes,
    39→            update_interval=update_interval,
    40→            config_entry=config_entry,
    41→        )
    42→        self.data: dict[int, int | str] = {}
    43→
    44→        # Set up drift detection timer for locks with hard_refresh_interval
    45→        if lock.hard_refresh_interval:
    46→            self._drift_unsub = async_track_time_interval(
    47→                hass,
    48→                self._async_drift_check,
    49→                lock.hard_refresh_interval,
    50→            )
    51→
    52→        if lock.connection_check_interval:
    53→            # Periodic connection checks drive reconnect handling for non-push providers.
    54→            self._connection_unsub = async_track_time_interval(
    55→                hass,
    56→                self._async_connection_check,
    57→                lock.connection_check_interval,
    58→            )
    59→
    60→    @property
    61→    def lock(self) -> BaseLock:
    62→        """Return the lock."""
    63→        return self._lock
    64→
    65→    @callback
    66→    def push_update(self, updates: dict[int, int | str]) -> None:
    67→        """Push one or more slot updates and notify listening entities."""
    68→        if not updates:
    69→            return
    70→
    71→        new_data = {**self.data, **updates}
    72→        # Skip update if data hasn't actually changed to avoid redundant logging
    73→        # and unnecessary listener notifications
    74→        if new_data == self.data:
    75→            return
    76→
    77→        self.async_set_updated_data(new_data)
    78→
    79→    async def async_get_usercodes(self) -> dict[int, int | str]:
    80→        """Update usercodes."""
    81→        try:
    82→            return await self._lock.async_internal_get_usercodes()
    83→        except LockCodeManagerError as err:
    84→            # We can silently fail if we've never been able to retrieve data
    85→            if not self.last_update_success:
    86→                return {}
    87→            raise UpdateFailed from err
    88→
    89→    async def _async_drift_check(self, now: datetime) -> None:
    90→        """
    91→        Perform periodic drift detection.
    92→
    93→        Hard refresh re-fetches codes from the lock to detect out-of-band changes
    94→        (e.g., codes changed at the lock's keypad). If changes are detected,
    95→        updates coordinator data and notifies listeners.
    96→        """
    97→        # Skip if we haven't successfully loaded initial data yet
    98→        if not self.last_update_success:
    99→            return
   100→
   101→        _LOGGER.debug(
   102→            "Performing drift detection hard refresh for %s",
   103→            self._lock.lock.entity_id,
   104→        )
   105→        try:
   106→            new_data = await self._lock.async_internal_hard_refresh_codes()
   107→        except LockCodeManagerError as err:
   108→            _LOGGER.warning(
   109→                "Drift detection hard refresh failed for %s: %s",
   110→                self._lock.lock.entity_id,
   111→                err,
   112→            )
   113→            return
   114→
   115→        # Retry push subscription if supported but not yet subscribed
   116→        # (handles case where initial setup failed but lock is now available)
   117→        if self._lock.supports_push:
   118→            self._lock.subscribe_push_updates()
   119→
   120→        # Compare with current data and notify if changed
   121→        if new_data != self.data:
   122→            _LOGGER.debug(
   123→                "Drift detected for %s, updating coordinator data",
   124→                self._lock.lock.entity_id,
   125→            )
   126→            self.async_set_updated_data(new_data)
   127→
   128→    async def _async_connection_check(self, now: datetime) -> None:
   129→        """Poll connection state so providers can resubscribe on reconnect."""
   130→        try:
   131→            await self._lock.async_internal_is_connection_up()
   132→        except LockCodeManagerError as err:
   133→            _LOGGER.debug(
   134→                "Connection check failed for %s: %s", self._lock.lock.entity_id, err
   135→            )
   136→
   137→    async def async_shutdown(self) -> None:
   138→        """Shut down the coordinator and clean up resources."""
   139→        if self._drift_unsub:
   140→            self._drift_unsub()
   141→            self._drift_unsub = None
   142→        if self._connection_unsub:
   143→            self._connection_unsub()
   144→            self._connection_unsub = None
   145→        await super().async_shutdown()

   500→        )

   502→    async def _perform_sync_operation(self, slot_state: _SlotState) -> bool:
   503→        """
   504→        Perform sync operation (set or clear usercode).
   505→
   506→        Returns True if sync was performed, False if lock disconnected.
   507→        """
   508→        try:
   509→            if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON:
   510→                await self.lock.async_internal_set_usercode(
   511→                    int(self.slot_num), slot_state.pin_state, slot_state.name_state
   512→                )
   513→                _LOGGER.debug(
   514→                    "%s (%s): Set usercode for %s slot %s",
   515→                    self.config_entry.entry_id,
   516→                    self.config_entry.title,
   517→                    self.lock.lock.entity_id,
   518→                    self.slot_num,
   519→                )
   520→            else:  # active_state == STATE_OFF
   521→                await self.lock.async_internal_clear_usercode(int(self.slot_num))
   522→                _LOGGER.debug(
   523→                    "%s (%s): Cleared usercode for %s slot %s",
   524→                    self.config_entry.entry_id,
   525→                    self.config_entry.title,
   526→                    self.lock.lock.entity_id,
   527→                    self.slot_num,
   528→                )
   529→            self._cancel_retry()
   530→            return True
   531→        except LockDisconnected as err:
   532→            _LOGGER.debug(
   533→                "%s (%s): Unable to %s usercode for %s slot %s: %s",
   534→                self.config_entry.entry_id,
   535→                self.config_entry.title,
   536→                "set" if slot_state.active_state == STATE_ON else "clear",
   537→                self.lock.lock.entity_id,
   538→                self.slot_num,
   539→                err,
   540→            )
   541→            self._schedule_retry()

   544→    async def _async_update_state(
   545→        self, event: Event[EventStateChangedData] | None = None

   547→        """
   548→        Update binary sensor state by checking dependent entity states.
   549→
   550→        On initial load (when _attr_is_on is None): Sets sync state without operations.
   551→        On subsequent updates: Performs sync operations when out of sync.
   552→        """
   553→        async with self._lock:
   554→            slot_state = self._resolve_slot_state(event)
   555→            if slot_state is None:
   556→                return
   557→
   558→            # Calculate expected sync state
   559→            expected_in_sync = self._calculate_expected_sync(slot_state)

   562→            if self._attr_is_on is None:
   563→                # Guard: Verify active state is valid
   564→                if slot_state.active_state not in (STATE_ON, STATE_OFF):

   570→                        self.slot_num,
   571→                        slot_state.active_state,
   572→                    )
   573→                    return
   574→
   575→                self._update_sync_state(expected_in_sync)

   577→                    "%s (%s): Initial state loaded for %s slot %s, in_sync=%s",

   369→    def _setup_config_entry_state_listener(self) -> None:

   393→            ):

   395→
   396→            self._last_entry_state = to_state
   397→

   399→            _handle_state_change
   400→        )

   463→    def set_usercode(
   464→        self, code_slot: int, usercode: int | str, name: str | None = None
   465→    ) -> bool:
   466→        """
   467→        Set a usercode on a code slot.
   468→
   469→        Returns True if the value was changed, False if already set to this value.
   470→        If the provider cannot determine whether a change occurred, return True
   471→        to ensure the coordinator refreshes and verifies the state.
   472→
   473→        Optimistic Updates
   474→        ------------------
   475→        For providers where the underlying integration's value cache updates
   476→        asynchronously (e.g., Z-Wave JS push notifications), the base class
   477→        coordinator refresh may read stale data, causing sync loops.
   478→
   479→        In these cases, call ``self.coordinator.push_update({code_slot: usercode})``
   480→        after a successful set operation to update coordinator data immediately.
   481→        This prevents the sync sensor from seeing a mismatch and retrying.
   482→
   483→        Providers with synchronous caches (like Virtual) don't need this as
   484→        get_usercodes() returns the updated value immediately.
   485→
   486→        Raises:
   487→            LockDisconnected: If the lock cannot be communicated with.
   488→
   489→        """
   490→        self._raise_not_implemented(
   491→            "set_usercode",
   492→            "Override this method to set a usercode on the lock.",
   493→        )
   494→
   495→    async def async_set_usercode(
   496→        self, code_slot: int, usercode: int | str, name: str | None = None
   497→    ) -> bool:
   498→        """
   499→        Set a usercode on a code slot.
   500→
   501→        Returns True if the value was changed, False if already set to this value.
   502→        If the provider cannot determine whether a change occurred, return True
   503→        to ensure the coordinator refreshes and verifies the state.
   504→        """
   505→        return await self._async_executor_call(
   506→            self.set_usercode, code_slot, usercode, name=name
   507→        )
   508→
   509→    @final
   510→    async def async_internal_set_usercode(
   511→        self, code_slot: int, usercode: int | str, name: str | None = None
   512→    ) -> None:
   513→        """Set a usercode on a code slot."""
   514→        changed = await self._execute_rate_limited(
   515→            "set", self.async_set_usercode, code_slot, usercode, name=name
   516→        )
   517→        # Refresh coordinator to update entity states from cache (only if changed).
   518→        # Skip for push-based providers — they update the coordinator optimistically
   519→        # via push_update() in their set/clear methods, and refreshing from cache
   520→        # could overwrite the optimistic update with stale data when the underlying
   521→        # driver defers cache updates until device confirmation.
   522→        if changed and self.coordinator and not self.supports_push:
   523→            await self.coordinator.async_request_refresh()

   529→        Returns True if the value was changed, False if already cleared.

    52→class BaseLock:
    53→    """
    54→    Base class for lock provider implementations.
    55→
    56→    Data Fetching Modes
    57→    -------------------
    58→    The coordinator supports three update modes. All modes include an initial poll.
    59→
    60→    1. Poll for updates (default):
    61→       - Periodic calls to get_usercodes() at usercode_scan_interval
    62→       - Used when supports_push = False
    63→       - Suitable for integrations without real-time events
    64→
    65→    2. Push for updates:
    66→       - Real-time value updates via subscribe_push_updates()
    67→       - Enabled when supports_push = True
    68→       - Disables periodic polling (poll for updates)
    69→       - Updates pushed via coordinator.push_update({slot: value})
    70→
    71→    3. Poll for drift:
    72→       - Periodic hard_refresh_codes() at hard_refresh_interval
    73→       - Detects out-of-band changes (e.g., codes changed at keypad)
    74→       - Runs regardless of push/poll mode
    75→       - Set hard_refresh_interval = None to disable
    76→
    77→    4. Poll connection state:
    78→       - Periodic async_internal_is_connection_up() at connection_check_interval
    79→       - Helps detect reconnects for integrations without config entry state signals
    80→       - Set connection_check_interval = None to disable
    81→
    82→    Configuration Examples
    83→    ----------------------
    84→    Poll-only (default):
    85→        supports_push = False
    86→        usercode_scan_interval = timedelta(minutes=1)
    87→        hard_refresh_interval = None
    88→        connection_check_interval = timedelta(seconds=30)
    89→
    90→    Push with drift detection (recommended for Z-Wave JS):
    91→        supports_push = True
    92→        hard_refresh_interval = timedelta(hours=1)
    93→        connection_check_interval = None
    94→        # Override subscribe_push_updates() to handle value events
    95→
    96→    Poll with drift detection:
    97→        supports_push = False
    98→        usercode_scan_interval = timedelta(minutes=1)
    99→        hard_refresh_interval = timedelta(hours=1)
   100→        connection_check_interval = timedelta(seconds=30)

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/tests/providers/test_zwave_js.py",
  "old_string": "@pytest.fixture\ndef mock_zwave_usercodes():\n    \"\"\"Mock Z-Wave JS usercode functions for the lifetime of the test.\n\n    Keeps both ``get_usercodes`` and ``get_usercode`` returning consistent\n    data so the coordinator and sync logic don't make real Z-Wave calls.\n    \"\"\"\n    with (\n        patch(\n            \"custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercodes\",\n        ) as mock_all,\n        patch(\n            \"custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercode\",\n        ) as mock_one,\n    ):\n        mock_all.return_value = []\n        mock_one.return_value = {\"in_use\": False, \"usercode\": \"\"}\n        yield mock_all, mock_one\n\n\nasync def _setup_lcm_entry(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    lock_entity_id: str,\n    slots: dict[str, dict],\n    mock_zwave_usercodes: tuple[MagicMock, MagicMock],\n) -> MockConfigEntry:\n    \"\"\"Set up a full LCM config entry with real platform entities.\"\"\"\n    mock_all, mock_one = mock_zwave_usercodes\n\n    cache_entries = []\n    usercode_by_slot: dict[int, dict] = {}\n    for k, v in slots.items():\n        pin = v.get(CONF_PIN, \"\")\n        in_use = bool(pin)\n        cache_entries.append(\n            {\"code_slot\": int(k), \"usercode\": pin, \"in_use\": in_use}\n        )\n        usercode_by_slot[int(k)] = {\"in_use\": in_use, \"usercode\": pin}\n\n    mock_all.return_value = cache_entries\n    mock_one.side_effect = lambda node, slot: usercode_by_slot.get(\n        slot, {\"in_use\": False, \"usercode\": \"\"}\n    )\n\n    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(\n        domain=DOMAIN,\n        data={\n            CONF_LOCKS: [lock_entity_id],\n            CONF_SLOTS: slots,\n        },\n        unique_id=f\"duplicate_code_test_{lock_entity_id}\",\n    )\n    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n    await hass.config_entries.async_setup(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n    await hass.async_block_till_done()\n    return lcm_entry",
  "new_string": "@pytest.fixture\ndef mock_zwave_usercodes(zwave_client: MagicMock):\n    \"\"\"Mock Z-Wave JS usercode functions with mutable state.\n\n    Both ``get_usercodes`` and ``get_usercode`` read from a shared mutable\n    ``codes`` dict.  The client's ``async_send_command`` is wrapped so that\n    set / clear Z-Wave commands automatically update ``codes``, preventing\n    the coordinator refresh from overwriting optimistic push updates with\n    stale data.\n\n    Yields ``(mock_get_usercodes, mock_get_usercode, codes)`` where *codes*\n    is ``dict[int, dict]`` keyed by slot number.\n    \"\"\"\n    codes: dict[int, dict] = {}\n\n    original_side_effect = zwave_client.async_send_command.side_effect\n\n    async def _send_command_with_codes(message, require_schema=None):\n        if message.get(\"command\") == \"node.set_value\":\n            vid = message.get(\"valueId\", {})\n            if vid.get(\"commandClass\") == CommandClass.USER_CODE:\n                slot = vid.get(\"propertyKey\")\n                if slot is not None:\n                    prop = vid.get(\"property\")\n                    if prop == \"userIdStatus\":\n                        # Clear operation\n                        codes[slot] = {\n                            \"code_slot\": slot,\n                            \"in_use\": False,\n                            \"usercode\": \"\",\n                        }\n                    elif prop == \"userCode\":\n                        # Set operation\n                        codes[slot] = {\n                            \"code_slot\": slot,\n                            \"in_use\": True,\n                            \"usercode\": str(message[\"value\"]),\n                        }\n        return await original_side_effect(message, require_schema)\n\n    with (\n        patch(\n            \"custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercodes\",\n        ) as mock_all,\n        patch(\n            \"custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercode\",\n        ) as mock_one,\n    ):\n        mock_all.side_effect = lambda node: list(codes.values())\n        mock_one.side_effect = lambda node, slot: codes.get(\n            slot, {\"code_slot\": slot, \"in_use\": False, \"usercode\": \"\"}\n        )\n        zwave_client.async_send_command.side_effect = _send_command_with_codes\n        yield mock_all, mock_one, codes\n        zwave_client.async_send_command.side_effect = original_side_effect\n\n\nasync def _setup_lcm_entry(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    lock_entity_id: str,\n    slots: dict[str, dict],\n    mock_zwave_usercodes: tuple[MagicMock, MagicMock, dict[int, dict]],\n) -> MockConfigEntry:\n    \"\"\"Set up a full LCM config entry with real platform entities.\"\"\"\n    _mock_all, _mock_one, codes = mock_zwave_usercodes\n\n    for k, v in slots.items():\n        pin = v.get(CONF_PIN, \"\")\n        in_use = bool(pin)\n        codes[int(k)] = {\"code_slot\": int(k), \"usercode\": pin, \"in_use\": in_use}\n\n    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(\n        domain=DOMAIN,\n        data={\n            CONF_LOCKS: [lock_entity_id],\n            CONF_SLOTS: slots,\n        },\n        unique_id=f\"duplicate_code_test_{lock_entity_id}\",\n    )\n    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n    await hass.config_entries.async_setup(lcm_entry.entry_id)\n    await hass.async_block_till_done()\n    return lcm_entry"
}

{

  "old_string": "async def test_duplicate_code_notification_ignored_when_user_id_mismatches(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,\n    zwave_integration: MockConfigEntry,\n    lock_schlage_be469: Node,\n    mock_zwave_usercodes: tuple[MagicMock, MagicMock],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Test event 15 is ignored when userId doesn't match in-progress slot.\"\"\"\n    lcm_entry = await _setup_lcm_entry(\n        hass,\n        zwave_js_lock.lock.entity_id,\n        {\n            \"2\": {CONF_NAME: \"test2\", CONF_PIN: \"1234\", CONF_ENABLED: True},\n            \"3\": {CONF_NAME: \"test3\", CONF_PIN: \"5678\", CONF_ENABLED: True},\n        },\n        mock_zwave_usercodes,\n    )\n    switch_2 = _get_enabled_switch_entity_id(hass, lcm_entry.entry_id, 2)\n    switch_3 = _get_enabled_switch_entity_id(hass, lcm_entry.entry_id, 3)\n\n    runtime_data: LockCodeManagerConfigEntryData = lcm_entry.runtime_data\n    lock_instance = runtime_data.locks[zwave_js_lock.lock.entity_id]\n\n    # LCM is setting slot 2, but notification says slot 3\n    lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot = 2\n\n    lock_schlage_be469.receive_event(\n        _make_duplicate_code_event(lock_schlage_be469.node_id, user_id=3)\n    )\n    await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n    # Neither switch should be turned off\n    assert hass.states.get(switch_2).state == STATE_ON\n    assert hass.states.get(switch_3).state == STATE_ON\n\n    # In-progress should NOT be cleared (it wasn't our event)\n    assert lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot == 2\n\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(lcm_entry.entry_id)",
  "new_string": "async def test_duplicate_code_notification_ignored_when_user_id_mismatches(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    zwave_integration: MockConfigEntry,\n    lock_entity: er.RegistryEntry,\n    lock_schlage_be469: Node,\n    mock_zwave_usercodes: tuple[MagicMock, MagicMock, dict[int, dict]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Test event 15 is ignored when userId doesn't match in-progress slot.\"\"\"\n    lcm_entry = await _setup_lcm_entry(\n        hass,\n        lock_entity.entity_id,\n        {\n            \"2\": {CONF_NAME: \"test2\", CONF_PIN: \"1234\", CONF_ENABLED: True},\n            \"3\": {CONF_NAME: \"test3\", CONF_PIN: \"5678\", CONF_ENABLED: True},\n        },\n        mock_zwave_usercodes,\n    )\n    switch_2 = _get_enabled_switch_entity_id(hass, lcm_entry.entry_id, 2)\n    switch_3 = _get_enabled_switch_entity_id(hass, lcm_entry.entry_id, 3)\n\n    runtime_data: LockCodeManagerConfigEntryData = lcm_entry.runtime_data\n    lock_instance = runtime_data.locks[lock_entity.entity_id]\n\n    # LCM is setting slot 2, but notification says slot 3\n    lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot = 2\n\n    lock_schlage_be469.receive_event(\n        _make_duplicate_code_event(lock_schlage_be469.node_id, user_id=3)\n    )\n    await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n    # Neither switch should be turned off\n    assert hass.states.get(switch_2).state == STATE_ON\n    assert hass.states.get(switch_3).state == STATE_ON\n\n    # In-progress should NOT be cleared (it wasn't our event)\n    assert lock_instance._set_in_progress_code_slot == 2\n\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(lcm_entry.entry_id)"

+@pytest.fixture
+def mock_zwave_usercodes(zwave_client: MagicMock):
+    """Mock Z-Wave JS usercode functions with mutable state.
+
+    Both ``get_usercodes`` and ``get_usercode`` read from a shared mutable
+    ``codes`` dict.  The client's ``async_send_command`` is wrapped so that
+    set / clear Z-Wave commands automatically update ``codes``, preventing
+    the coordinator refresh from overwriting optimistic push updates with
+    stale data.
+
+    Yields ``(mock_get_usercodes, mock_get_usercode, codes)`` where *codes*
+    is ``dict[int, dict]`` keyed by slot number.
+    """
+    codes: dict[int, dict] = {}
+
+    original_side_effect = zwave_client.async_send_command.side_effect
+
+    async def _send_command_with_codes(message, require_schema=None):
+        if message.get("command") == "node.set_value":
+            vid = message.get("valueId", {})
+            if vid.get("commandClass") == CommandClass.USER_CODE:
+                slot = vid.get("propertyKey")
+                if slot is not None:
+                    prop = vid.get("property")
+                    if prop == "userIdStatus":
+                        # Clear operation
+                        codes[slot] = {
+                            "code_slot": slot,
+                            "in_use": False,
+                            "usercode": "",
+                        }
+                    elif prop == "userCode":
+                        # Set operation
+                        codes[slot] = {
+                            "code_slot": slot,
+                            "in_use": True,
+                            "usercode": str(message["value"]),
+                        }
+        return await original_side_effect(message, require_schema)
+
+    with (
+        patch(
+            "custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercodes",
+        ) as mock_all,
+        patch(
+            "custom_components.lock_code_manager.providers.zwave_js.get_usercode",
+        ) as mock_one,
+    ):
+        mock_all.side_effect = lambda node: list(codes.values())
+        mock_one.side_effect = lambda node, slot: codes.get(
+            slot, {"code_slot": slot, "in_use": False, "usercode": ""}
+        )
+        zwave_client.async_send_command.side_effect = _send_command_with_codes
+        yield mock_all, mock_one, codes
+        zwave_client.async_send_command.side_effect = original_side_effect
+
+
+async def _setup_lcm_entry(
+    hass: HomeAssistant,
+    lock_entity_id: str,
+    slots: dict[str, dict],
+    mock_zwave_usercodes: tuple[MagicMock, MagicMock, dict[int, dict]],
+) -> MockConfigEntry:
+    """Set up a full LCM config entry with real platform entities."""
+    _mock_all, _mock_one, codes = mock_zwave_usercodes
+
+    for k, v in slots.items():
+        pin = v.get(CONF_PIN, "")
+        in_use = bool(pin)
+        codes[int(k)] = {"code_slot": int(k), "usercode": pin, "in_use": in_use}
+
+    lcm_entry = MockConfigEntry(
+        domain=DOMAIN,
+        data={
+            CONF_LOCKS: [lock_entity_id],
+            CONF_SLOTS: slots,
+        },
+        unique_id=f"duplicate_code_test_{lock_entity_id}",
+    )
+    lcm_entry.add_to_hass(hass)
+    await hass.config_entries.async_setup(lcm_entry.entry_id)
+    await hass.async_block_till_done()
+    return lcm_entry
+
+
+def _get_enabled_switch_entity_id(hass: HomeAssistant, entry_id: str, slot: int) -> str:
+    """Get the enabled switch entity ID for a slot."""
+    ent_reg = er.async_get(hass)
+    uid = f"{entry_id}|{slot}|{CONF_ENABLED}"
+    entity_id = ent_reg.async_get_entity_id("switch", DOMAIN, uid)
+    assert entity_id, f"Switch entity not found for slot {slot}"
+    return entity_id
+
+

> AGENT

Done. Here's a summary of what was fixed:

**Root cause**: The duplicate code tests were hanging because after `_async_handle_duplicate_code` turned off the switch, the sync loop in `binary_sensor.py` detected the code was out of sync (switch OFF but code still on lock), tried to clear it via Z-Wave service, then `coordinator.async_refresh()` called `get_usercodes()` which returned the same stale mock data, overwriting the optimistic push update - creating an infinite sync loop.

**Fix**: Made `mock_zwave_usercodes` fixture use a **mutable state dict** that both `get_usercodes`/`get_usercode` read from AND that automatically updates when Z-Wave set/clear commands flow through `async_send_command`. This keeps the mock "lock state" consistent with the operations performed on it.

**Changes in this commit**:
- `zwave_js.py`: `async _async_handle_duplicate_code()` using `switch.turn_off` via `asyncio.gather`, dispatched from sync callback via `hass.async_create_task`
- `tests/conftest.py`: `patch.dict` preserving real `INTEGRATIONS_CLASS_MAP` entries
- `tests/providers/test_zwave_js.py`: Full LCM platform setup tests with mutable mock state, removed `zwave_js_lock` fixture dependency from duplicate code tests

PR #899 has been force-pushed with the rebased branch (now based on `main`, not `fix/coordinator-backoff`).

> DEVELOPER

has the logic changed at all in my change?

> AGENT

Looking at the `conftest.py` change:

```python
# Before
with patch(
    "custom_components.lock_code_manager.helpers.INTEGRATIONS_CLASS_MAP",
    {"test": MockLCMLock},
):

# After
with patch.dict(
    "custom_components.lock_code_manager.helpers.INTEGRATIONS_CLASS_MAP",
    {"test": MockLCMLock, **INTEGRATIONS_CLASS_MAP},
):
```

**Yes, the logic changed.** Before, the patch **replaced** the entire dict with only `{"test": MockLCMLock}`, removing all real providers (like `zwave_js`). Now it **adds** `MockLCMLock` alongside the real providers.

This is what made the full LCM setup tests possible — without real providers in the map, `hass.config_entries.async_setup` couldn't create `ZWaveJSLock` instances for Z-Wave JS lock entities.

Is this the change you're asking about, or were you asking about a different file?

1. **How slots are disabled**: Config entry data mutation (`entry.data[slot][CONF_ENABLED] = False`) → `switch.turn_off` service calls via `asyncio.gather`
2. **Sync → async**: `@callback _handle_duplicate_code()` called directly → `async _async_handle_duplicate_code()` dispatched via `hass.async_create_task()`
3. **Removed `copy` import**, added `asyncio`, `SWITCH_DOMAIN`, `SERVICE_TURN_OFF`
4. **Simplified log/notification text**: No longer lists affected config entry names (since notification is per-lock, not per-entry)

All of these were explicitly requested by you in the prior session.

> AGENT

Your changes alter the logic in a few ways:

1. **Single entry instead of all entries**: `next(... for entry in ...)` finds only the **first** matching config entry, whereas the committed code iterates **all** entries and disables the slot in each one. If multiple LCM config entries manage the same lock+slot, yours would only disable one.

2. **No `asyncio.gather`**: Single service call instead of concurrent calls to multiple switches (follows from #1).

3. **Shared message helper**: `_dupe_msg` DRYs up the log and notification text — same message content, no logic change there.

4. **Removed `import asyncio`**: Follows from dropping `asyncio.gather`.

5. **Early return on no match**: `StopIteration` → `return` is new defensive behavior (committed code would just skip the loop and still log + create notification even with no matching entries).

So the key question is: **can multiple LCM config entries manage the same lock and slot?** If yes, the committed version is correct. If that's impossible by design, your version is simpler and equivalent.

> AGENT

The issue is `get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {})` returns the slots dict. Iterating a dict yields its keys (strings like `"2"`). `int(s)` converts those to ints. But the error says `'bool' object is not iterable` — that means `get_entry_data` is returning a bool instead of a dict somewhere.

Actually, looking more carefully: `CONF_SLOTS` is `"slots"` and `CONF_ENABLED` is `"enabled"`. The config data has `{"slots": {"2": {"name": "test", "pin": "1234", "enabled": True}}}`. `get_entry_data(entry, CONF_SLOTS, {})` should return the slots dict. Let me check `get_entry_data`.

> AGENT

Walrus operator precedence bug. The `:=` has lower precedence than `is`, so `enabled_ent_id` is being assigned `True`/`False` (the result of `... is None`), not the entity ID.

> TOOL

> AGENT

The `_dupe_msg("%s", "%s")` produces a string with three `%s` placeholders (slot appears 3 times), but only 2 args are passed to `_LOGGER.error`. Fix: pass `code_slot` three times, or better, just use f-string directly in the log.

> TOOL
